{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"call_67735b3bd62a43f0863dce7c","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# bead-forge (`bf`)\n2\t\n3\t![bead-forge hero](hero.png)\n4\t\n5\t**bead-forge** is a Rust CLI that replaces `br` (beads_rust) as the bead management tool for AI-supervised coding workflows. It is a drop-in replacement for `br` — every command, flag, and output format is identical — with one critical improvement: **concurrent claiming is correct**.\n6\t\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## The Problem It Solves\n10\t\n11\t`br` has a race condition at its core. Claiming a bead requires two separate operations:\n12\t\n13\t```\n14\tbr ready --json          # read candidates (snapshot)\n15\t  ↓  (race window)\n16\tbr update <id> --status in_progress --assignee <worker>\n17\t  ↓  (race window)\n18\tbr show <id>             # verify the claim stuck\n19\t```\n20\t\n21\tWith 20 NEEDLE workers running simultaneously, multiple workers read the same candidate list, all select the same top bead, and all issue `br update`. SQLite last-writer-wins — 19 workers hold phantom claims on beads they don't actually own.\n22\t\n23\t`bf` eliminates this with a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` SQLite transaction:\n24\t\n25\t```\n26\tbf claim --assignee worker-7 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --json\n27\t```\n28\t\n29\tThe entire read-score-update sequence runs inside one write lock. No two workers can observe the same candidate state.\n30\t\n31\t---\n32\t\n33\t## Architecture\n34\t\n35\t```\n36\tSQLite (live store)                    issues.jsonl (git artifact)\n37\t    │                                        │\n38\t    │  all reads and writes                  │  exported by bf sync --flush\n39\t    │                                        │  imported by bf sync --import\n40\t    ▼                                        ▼\n41\t  beads.db                            .beads/issues.jsonl\n42\t  (gitignored)                        (committed to git)\n43\t```\n44\t\n45\t**SQLite is the live database.** JSONL is a derived snapshot committed to git for backup, cross-machine sharing, and recovery. `beads.db` is rebuilt at any time with `bf doctor --repair`.\n46\t\n47\t**No daemon. No server. No flock.** Each `bf` invocation is self-contained: open DB, acquire write lock, execute, release, exit. SQLite WAL mode enables concurrent reads during writes.\n48\t\n49\t---\n50\t\n51\t## Key Features\n52\t\n53\t### Atomic Concurrent Claiming\n54\t\n55\t```\n56\tWorker 1: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT + score → UPDATE winner → COMMIT\n57\tWorker 2: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked until Worker 1 commits) → SELECT → ...\n58\t...\n59\tWorker 20: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT → NONE (queue empty) → COMMIT\n60\t```\n61\t\n62\tThe entire claim pipeline — stale reclamation, candidate scoring, winner selection, status update, event recording — runs in a single SQLite write transaction. Guaranteed correct under any concurrency level.\n63\t\n64\t### Critical Path Scoring\n65\t\n66\t`bf` computes the **float** of each bead: how many hops it can slip before delaying its epic. Beads on the critical path (`float == 0`) receive a 1000-point bonus in the claim scorer, ensuring the fleet works on the most impactful beads automatically — without human priority curation.\n67\t\n68\t```\n69\t$ bf critical-path bf-epic-123\n70\t\n71\t  float=0  [bf-a3f8] Implement auth token refresh        in_progress\n72\t  float=0  [bf-b2c1] Fix concurrent session state bug    open\n73\t  float=2  [bf-d5e3] Update API documentation            open\n74\t```\n75\t\n76\t### Velocity-Aware Scoring\n77\t\n78\tWorkers declare their composition when claiming:\n79\t\n80\t```bash\n81\tbf claim --assignee worker-7 \\\n82\t         --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n83\t         --harness needle \\\n84\t         --harness-version 0.5.2 \\\n85\t         --json\n86\t```\n87\t\n88\t`bf` tracks close times per `(model, harness, issue_type)` in `velocity_stats`. The claim scorer weights candidates by `impact / expected_duration` — the fleet maximizes throughput per unit time, not just raw priority. After 10+ completions per cohort, routing becomes measurably smarter.\n89\t\n90\t```\n91\t$ bf velocity\n92\t\n93\tModel                  Harness   Type     Samples  p50    p90\n94\tclaude-opus-4-7        needle    task     142      8m     22m\n95\tclaude-sonnet-4-6      needle    task     87       18m    45m\n96\tclaude-haiku-4-5       needle    task     23       35m    2h10m\n97\t```\n98\t\n99\t### Atomic Batch Operations\n100\t\n101\tReplaces NEEDLE's crash-unsafe create+dep chains:\n102\t\n103\t```bash\n104\t# NEEDLE mitosis: split 1 bead into N children atomically\n105\t# Method 1: Dedicated mitosis command (recommended)\n106\tbf mitosis bf-a3f8 \\\n107\t  --children '[\n108\t    {\"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2},\n109\t    {\"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2}\n110\t  ]' \\\n111\t  --reason \"Split into children\"\n112\t\n113\t# Method 2: Direct batch with placeholder references\n114\t# Use @0, @1, ... to reference beads created earlier in the batch\n115\tbf batch --json '[\n116\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n117\t  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n118\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@0\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n119\t  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@1\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n120\t  {\"op\": \"close\", \"id\": \"bf-a3f8\", \"reason\": \"Split into children\"}\n121\t]'\n122\t```\n123\t\n124\t**Placeholder references**: `@0`, `@1`, etc. resolve to the IDs of beads created earlier in the batch. You don't need to know the child IDs in advance — `bf` substitutes them automatically.\n125\t\n126\tAll operations execute in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. A crash mid-batch leaves zero partial state — SQLite rolls back automatically.\n127\t\n128\t### Extensible Annotations\n129\t\n130\tArbitrary key-value metadata on any bead, transparent to `br`:\n131\t\n132\t```bash\n133\tbf annotate bf-a3f8 needle_attempt=3 needle_session=abc123 review_status=needs_review\n134\tbf list --annotation needle_attempt=3\n135\t```\n136\t\n137\t### Operation History\n138\t\n139\t```bash\n140\tbf log bf-a3f8\n141\t\n142\t2026-04-29 10:00  CREATED     by: human                  \"Implement auth flow\"\n143\t2026-04-29 14:30  CLAIMED     by: worker-3 (sonnet-4-6)  open → in_progress\n144\t2026-04-29 15:45  COMMENT     by: worker-3               \"Found edge case\"\n145\t2026-04-29 16:00  CLOSED      by: worker-3               \"Completed\"\n146\t```\n147\t\n148\t---\n149\t\n150\t### Secret Scanning (Pre-Commit Hook)\n151\t\n152\t`bf` scans for secrets before writing to the database (see `.beads/config.yaml` → `secret_protection`). The `bf commit-check` command extends this to git — it runs as a pre-commit hook to block commits that would add secrets to `.beads/` files.\n153\t\n154\t```bash\n155\t# Manual scan\n156\tbf commit-check\n157\t# Exits 0 (clean) or 1 (secrets found) with detailed error output\n158\t```\n159\t\n160\t**Installation as git pre-commit hook:**\n161\t\n162\t```bash\n163\t# In your workspace repo\n164\tcat > .git/hooks/pre-commit << 'EOF'\n165\t#!/bin/sh\n166\tbf commit-check\n167\tEOF\n168\t\n169\tchmod +x .git/hooks/pre-commit\n170\t```\n171\t\n172\t**What gets scanned:**\n173\t- `.beads/config.yaml` — workspace configuration\n174\t- `.beads/metadata.json` — database path settings\n175\t- `.beads/issues.jsonl` — bead data (git artifact)\n176\t\n177\t**Patterns detected:**\n178\t- AWS keys (`AKIA...`, secret access keys)\n179\t- GitHub tokens (`ghp_`, `gho_`, `github_pat_`)\n180\t- Private keys (`BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY`, `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY`)\n181\t- JWT tokens\n182\t- API keys in URLs\n183\t- Database connection strings\n184\t- And more (same scanner as write-time protection)\n185\t\n186\t**Allowlist exceptions:**\n187\t\n188\tIf a pattern is legitimately non-secret, add to `.beads/config.yaml`:\n189\t\n190\t```yaml\n191\tsecret_protection:\n192\t  allowlist:\n193\t    - \"^AKIAEXAMPLE$\"  # exact string\n194\t    - \"password: test\"  # substring\n195\t```\n196\t\n197\t---\n198\t\n199\t## Commands\n200\t\n201\t```\n202\tbf create        --title \"...\" --type <type> --priority <N> [--label ...]\n203\tbf list          [--status <s>] [--type <t>] [--assignee <a>] [--format json|text|toon]\n204\tbf show          <id>\n205\tbf update        <id> [--status <s>] [--assignee <a>] ...]\n206\tbf close         <id> [--reason \"...\"]\n207\tbf claim         --assignee <id> [--model <m>] [--harness <h>] [--harness-version <v>]\n208\t                 [--workspace <path>] [--any]\n209\tbf critical-path <epic-id>\n210\tbf batch         [--file ops.json] [--json '[...]']\n211\tbf annotate      <id> key=value [--remove key]\n212\tbf log           [<id>] [--since <date>] [--actor <a>] [--diff]\n213\tbf rotate        [--age-days N]\n214\tbf velocity      [--model <m>] [--harness <h>]\n215\tbf dep           add-blocker <blocker> <blockee>\n216\tbf dep           tree <id>\n217\tbf label         <id> add <label> [<label> ...]\n218\tbf sync          [--flush-only] [--import-only]\n219\tbf doctor        [--repair] [--check] [--reclaim-stale [--ttl <duration>]]\n220\tbf init          [--prefix <p>]\n221\tbf stats\n222\tbf search        <query>\n223\tbf commit-check  # git pre-commit hook for secret scanning\n224\t```\n225\t\n226\tAll `br` commands work identically. `bf` is a strict superset.\n227\t\n228\t---\n229\t\n230\t## NEEDLE Integration\n231\t\n232\tReplace the five non-atomic `br` chains in `bead_store/mod.rs`:\n233\t\n234\t| Old (racy) | New (atomic) |\n235\t|-----------|--------------|\n236\t| `br ready` → `br update` → `br show` (3 calls, 2 race windows) | `bf claim` (1 call, 1 transaction) |\n237\t| `br create` + `br dep add` (orphan if crash between) | `bf batch` (crash-safe, all-or-nothing) |\n238\t| `br show` just to get `.labels` field | `bf labels <id>` (direct `SELECT` on labels table) |\n239\t| N × `br label add` loops (N processes) | `bf label <id> add l1 l2 l3` (one transaction) |\n240\t\n241\tBackward compatibility — install `br` as a symlink:\n242\t\n243\t```bash\n244\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n245\t```\n246\t\n247\t---\n248\t\n249\t## Compatibility\n250\t\n251\t`bf` reads and writes the same SQLite schema and JSONL format as `br`. A workspace used by `bf` can be read by `br` and vice versa. Verified by three test suites (see plan §Compatibility Verification):\n252\t\n253\t1. **JSONL round-trip**: `br list --format json` == `bf list --format json` on the same workspace\n254\t2. **SQLite compat**: `bf` writes, `br` reads, `br doctor` passes\n255\t3. **Claim race**: 20 concurrent `bf claim` processes, 10 beads — exactly 10 claims succeed, no bead claimed twice\n256\t\n257\t---\n258\t\n259\t## Reliability\n260\t\n261\t- **rusqlite** (standard SQLite C library) — not FrankenSQLite, which had known corruption under concurrent writes\n262\t- **WAL mode** — concurrent reads never block writes; writers queue, not corrupt\n263\t- **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`** — acquires write lock before any reads; eliminates TOCTOU\n264\t- **`SQLITE_BUSY` retry** — exponential backoff up to 5 retries; converts contention spikes to short waits\n265\t- **JSONL always authoritative** — `bf doctor --repair` rebuilds `beads.db` from `issues.jsonl` at any time\n266\t\n267\t---\n268\t\n269\t## Implementation\n270\t\n271\tSee [`docs/plan/plan.md`](plan/plan.md) for the complete implementation plan including:\n272\t\n273\t- Exact SQLite schema (all 13 tables + indexes)\n274\t- `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` retry wrapper implementation\n275\t- Critical path CTE algorithm\n276\t- Velocity stats schema and claim scoring formula\n277\t- Compatibility verification test suites\n278\t- NEEDLE integration specifics (file, line numbers, before/after)\n279\t\n280\t---\n281\t\n282\t## Migration from br to bf\n283\t\n284\t### Per-Machine Installation\n285\t\n286\t```bash\n287\t# Install bf binary\n288\tcurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n289\t  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n290\t\n291\t# Drop-in replace br (all existing scripts work unchanged)\n292\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n293\t\n294\t# Verify installation\n295\tbf --version\n296\tbr --version  # should show same version\n297\t```\n298\t\n299\t### Per-Workspace Migration\n300\t\n301\t**Standard migration** (Path B: explicit, with backup and verification):\n302\t\n303\t```bash\n304\t# Migrate a single workspace\n305\tbf migrate --workspace /path/to/workspace\n306\t\n307\t# Or migrate all workspaces in a loop\n308\tfor workspace in \\\n309\t  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n310\t  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n311\t  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n312\t  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n313\t  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n314\t  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n315\t  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n316\t  bf migrate --workspace \"$workspace\"\n317\tdone\n318\t```\n319\t\n320\t**What `bf migrate` does:**\n321\t1. Applies schema migrations (creates bf-only tables via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`)\n324\t4. Primes critical_path_cache for all epics\n325\t5. Seeds `config.yaml` with bf-specific defaults if missing\n326\t6. Verifies forward compatibility (br can still open the database)\n327\t7. Verifies backward compatibility (`bf doctor --check` passes)\n328\t8. This is expected because bf adds a `content_hash` column to the issues table for sync optimization. The database remains fully functional — both `bf doctor` and `br doctor` will pass. The warning indicates that br sees an extra column, but all br operations continue to work correctly.\n428\t\n429\t### NEEDLE Integration Update\n430\t\n431\tAfter migration, update NEEDLE adapter configs to pass worker metadata for velocity tracking:\n432\t\n433\t```yaml\n434\t# In .config/needle/adapters/claude-sonnet.yaml, update invoke_template:\n435\tbf claim --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --harness-version 0.5.2 ...\n436\t```\n437\t\n438\tThis enables velocity-aware routing (see §Velocity-Aware Scoring above).\n439\t\n440\t---\n441\t\n442\t## Build & Deploy\n443\t\n444\tBuilt via Argo Workflows on `iad-ci`. WorkflowTemplate: `bead-forge-build` in `jedarden/declarative-config`.\n445\t\n446\t```bash\n447\t# Install\n448\tcurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n449\t  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n450\t\n451\t# Drop-in replace br\n452\tln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n453\t\n454\t# Verify\n455\tbf --version\n456\tbf init --prefix bf\n457\tbf create \"first bead\" --type task\n458\t```\n459\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f58cec74-3f31-4493-9253-01b3e57dc6d9","uuid":"9fdf63fb-3b1a-43df-a196-f27bdceddf6d","timestamp":"2026-05-13T22:56:19.816Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/coding/bead-forge/docs/README.md","content":"# bead-forge (`bf`)\n\n![bead-forge hero](hero.png)\n\n**bead-forge** is a Rust CLI that replaces `br` (beads_rust) as the bead management tool for AI-supervised coding workflows. It is a drop-in replacement for `br` — every command, flag, and output format is identical — with one critical improvement: **concurrent claiming is correct**.\n\n---\n\n## The Problem It Solves\n\n`br` has a race condition at its core. Claiming a bead requires two separate operations:\n\n```\nbr ready --json          # read candidates (snapshot)\n  ↓  (race window)\nbr update <id> --status in_progress --assignee <worker>\n  ↓  (race window)\nbr show <id>             # verify the claim stuck\n```\n\nWith 20 NEEDLE workers running simultaneously, multiple workers read the same candidate list, all select the same top bead, and all issue `br update`. SQLite last-writer-wins — 19 workers hold phantom claims on beads they don't actually own.\n\n`bf` eliminates this with a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` SQLite transaction:\n\n```\nbf claim --assignee worker-7 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --json\n```\n\nThe entire read-score-update sequence runs inside one write lock. No two workers can observe the same candidate state.\n\n---\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nSQLite (live store)                    issues.jsonl (git artifact)\n    │                                        │\n    │  all reads and writes                  │  exported by bf sync --flush\n    │                                        │  imported by bf sync --import\n    ▼                                        ▼\n  beads.db                            .beads/issues.jsonl\n  (gitignored)                        (committed to git)\n```\n\n**SQLite is the live database.** JSONL is a derived snapshot committed to git for backup, cross-machine sharing, and recovery. `beads.db` is rebuilt at any time with `bf doctor --repair`.\n\n**No daemon. No server. No flock.** Each `bf` invocation is self-contained: open DB, acquire write lock, execute, release, exit. SQLite WAL mode enables concurrent reads during writes.\n\n---\n\n## Key Features\n\n### Atomic Concurrent Claiming\n\n```\nWorker 1: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT + score → UPDATE winner → COMMIT\nWorker 2: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked until Worker 1 commits) → SELECT → ...\n...\nWorker 20: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT → NONE (queue empty) → COMMIT\n```\n\nThe entire claim pipeline — stale reclamation, candidate scoring, winner selection, status update, event recording — runs in a single SQLite write transaction. Guaranteed correct under any concurrency level.\n\n### Critical Path Scoring\n\n`bf` computes the **float** of each bead: how many hops it can slip before delaying its epic. Beads on the critical path (`float == 0`) receive a 1000-point bonus in the claim scorer, ensuring the fleet works on the most impactful beads automatically — without human priority curation.\n\n```\n$ bf critical-path bf-epic-123\n\n  float=0  [bf-a3f8] Implement auth token refresh        in_progress\n  float=0  [bf-b2c1] Fix concurrent session state bug    open\n  float=2  [bf-d5e3] Update API documentation            open\n```\n\n### Velocity-Aware Scoring\n\nWorkers declare their composition when claiming:\n\n```bash\nbf claim --assignee worker-7 \\\n         --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n         --harness needle \\\n         --harness-version 0.5.2 \\\n         --json\n```\n\n`bf` tracks close times per `(model, harness, issue_type)` in `velocity_stats`. The claim scorer weights candidates by `impact / expected_duration` — the fleet maximizes throughput per unit time, not just raw priority. After 10+ completions per cohort, routing becomes measurably smarter.\n\n```\n$ bf velocity\n\nModel                  Harness   Type     Samples  p50    p90\nclaude-opus-4-7        needle    task     142      8m     22m\nclaude-sonnet-4-6      needle    task     87       18m    45m\nclaude-haiku-4-5       needle    task     23       35m    2h10m\n```\n\n### Atomic Batch Operations\n\nReplaces NEEDLE's crash-unsafe create+dep chains:\n\n```bash\n# NEEDLE mitosis: split 1 bead into N children atomically\n# Method 1: Dedicated mitosis command (recommended)\nbf mitosis bf-a3f8 \\\n  --children '[\n    {\"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2},\n    {\"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2}\n  ]' \\\n  --reason \"Split into children\"\n\n# Method 2: Direct batch with placeholder references\n# Use @0, @1, ... to reference beads created earlier in the batch\nbf batch --json '[\n  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@0\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@1\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n  {\"op\": \"close\", \"id\": \"bf-a3f8\", \"reason\": \"Split into children\"}\n]'\n```\n\n**Placeholder references**: `@0`, `@1`, etc. resolve to the IDs of beads created earlier in the batch. You don't need to know the child IDs in advance — `bf` substitutes them automatically.\n\nAll operations execute in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. A crash mid-batch leaves zero partial state — SQLite rolls back automatically.\n\n### Extensible Annotations\n\nArbitrary key-value metadata on any bead, transparent to `br`:\n\n```bash\nbf annotate bf-a3f8 needle_attempt=3 needle_session=abc123 review_status=needs_review\nbf list --annotation needle_attempt=3\n```\n\n### Operation History\n\n```bash\nbf log bf-a3f8\n\n2026-04-29 10:00  CREATED     by: human                  \"Implement auth flow\"\n2026-04-29 14:30  CLAIMED     by: worker-3 (sonnet-4-6)  open → in_progress\n2026-04-29 15:45  COMMENT     by: worker-3               \"Found edge case\"\n2026-04-29 16:00  CLOSED      by: worker-3               \"Completed\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Secret Scanning (Pre-Commit Hook)\n\n`bf` scans for secrets before writing to the database (see `.beads/config.yaml` → `secret_protection`). The `bf commit-check` command extends this to git — it runs as a pre-commit hook to block commits that would add secrets to `.beads/` files.\n\n```bash\n# Manual scan\nbf commit-check\n# Exits 0 (clean) or 1 (secrets found) with detailed error output\n```\n\n**Installation as git pre-commit hook:**\n\n```bash\n# In your workspace repo\ncat > .git/hooks/pre-commit << 'EOF'\n#!/bin/sh\nbf commit-check\nEOF\n\nchmod +x .git/hooks/pre-commit\n```\n\n**What gets scanned:**\n- `.beads/config.yaml` — workspace configuration\n- `.beads/metadata.json` — database path settings\n- `.beads/issues.jsonl` — bead data (git artifact)\n\n**Patterns detected:**\n- AWS keys (`AKIA...`, secret access keys)\n- GitHub tokens (`ghp_`, `gho_`, `github_pat_`)\n- Private keys (`BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY`, `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY`)\n- JWT tokens\n- API keys in URLs\n- Database connection strings\n- And more (same scanner as write-time protection)\n\n**Allowlist exceptions:**\n\nIf a pattern is legitimately non-secret, add to `.beads/config.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\nsecret_protection:\n  allowlist:\n    - \"^AKIAEXAMPLE$\"  # exact string\n    - \"password: test\"  # substring\n```\n\n---\n\n## Commands\n\n```\nbf create        --title \"...\" --type <type> --priority <N> [--label ...]\nbf list          [--status <s>] [--type <t>] [--assignee <a>] [--format json|text|toon]\nbf show          <id>\nbf update        <id> [--status <s>] [--assignee <a>] ...]\nbf close         <id> [--reason \"...\"]\nbf claim         --assignee <id> [--model <m>] [--harness <h>] [--harness-version <v>]\n                 [--workspace <path>] [--any]\nbf critical-path <epic-id>\nbf batch         [--file ops.json] [--json '[...]']\nbf annotate      <id> key=value [--remove key]\nbf log           [<id>] [--since <date>] [--actor <a>] [--diff]\nbf rotate        [--age-days N]\nbf velocity      [--model <m>] [--harness <h>]\nbf dep           add-blocker <blocker> <blockee>\nbf dep           tree <id>\nbf label         <id> add <label> [<label> ...]\nbf sync          [--flush-only] [--import-only]\nbf doctor        [--repair] [--check] [--reclaim-stale [--ttl <duration>]]\nbf init          [--prefix <p>]\nbf stats\nbf search        <query>\nbf commit-check  # git pre-commit hook for secret scanning\n```\n\nAll `br` commands work identically. `bf` is a strict superset.\n\n---\n\n## NEEDLE Integration\n\nReplace the five non-atomic `br` chains in `bead_store/mod.rs`:\n\n| Old (racy) | New (atomic) |\n|-----------|--------------|\n| `br ready` → `br update` → `br show` (3 calls, 2 race windows) | `bf claim` (1 call, 1 transaction) |\n| `br create` + `br dep add` (orphan if crash between) | `bf batch` (crash-safe, all-or-nothing) |\n| `br show` just to get `.labels` field | `bf labels <id>` (direct `SELECT` on labels table) |\n| N × `br label add` loops (N processes) | `bf label <id> add l1 l2 l3` (one transaction) |\n\nBackward compatibility — install `br` as a symlink:\n\n```bash\nln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n```\n\n---\n\n## Compatibility\n\n`bf` reads and writes the same SQLite schema and JSONL format as `br`. A workspace used by `bf` can be read by `br` and vice versa. Verified by three test suites (see plan §Compatibility Verification):\n\n1. **JSONL round-trip**: `br list --format json` == `bf list --format json` on the same workspace\n2. **SQLite compat**: `bf` writes, `br` reads, `br doctor` passes\n3. **Claim race**: 20 concurrent `bf claim` processes, 10 beads — exactly 10 claims succeed, no bead claimed twice\n\n---\n\n## Reliability\n\n- **rusqlite** (standard SQLite C library) — not FrankenSQLite, which had known corruption under concurrent writes\n- **WAL mode** — concurrent reads never block writes; writers queue, not corrupt\n- **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`** — acquires write lock before any reads; eliminates TOCTOU\n- **`SQLITE_BUSY` retry** — exponential backoff up to 5 retries; converts contention spikes to short waits\n- **JSONL always authoritative** — `bf doctor --repair` rebuilds `beads.db` from `issues.jsonl` at any time\n\n---\n\n## Implementation\n\nSee [`docs/plan/plan.md`](plan/plan.md) for the complete implementation plan including:\n\n- Exact SQLite schema (all 13 tables + indexes)\n- `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` retry wrapper implementation\n- Critical path CTE algorithm\n- Velocity stats schema and claim scoring formula\n- Compatibility verification test suites\n- NEEDLE integration specifics (file, line numbers, before/after)\n\n---\n\n## Migration from br to bf\n\n### Per-Machine Installation\n\n```bash\n# Install bf binary\ncurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n\n# Drop-in replace br (all existing scripts work unchanged)\nln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n\n# Verify installation\nbf --version\nbr --version  # should show same version\n```\n\n### Per-Workspace Migration\n\n**Standard migration** (Path B: explicit, with backup and verification):\n\n```bash\n# Migrate a single workspace\nbf migrate --workspace /path/to/workspace\n\n# Or migrate all workspaces in a loop\nfor workspace in \\\n  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n  bf migrate --workspace \"$workspace\"\ndone\n```\n\n**What `bf migrate` does:**\n1. Acquires migration lock (prevents concurrent claims during migration)\n2. Backs up `beads.db` → `beads.db.br-backup-<timestamp>`\n3. It is a drop-in replacement for `br` — every command, flag, and output format is identical — with one critical improvement: **concurrent claiming is correct**.\n\n---\n\n## The Problem It Solves\n\n`br` has a race condition at its core. Claiming a bead requires two separate operations:\n\n```\nbr ready --json          # read candidates (snapshot)\n  ↓  (race window)\nbr update <id> --status in_progress --assignee <worker>\n  ↓  (race window)\nbr show <id>             # verify the claim stuck\n```\n\nWith 20 NEEDLE workers running simultaneously, multiple workers read the same candidate list, all select the same top bead, and all issue `br update`. SQLite last-writer-wins — 19 workers hold phantom claims on beads they don't actually own.\n\n`bf` eliminates this with a single `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` SQLite transaction:\n\n```\nbf claim --assignee worker-7 --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --json\n```\n\nThe entire read-score-update sequence runs inside one write lock. No two workers can observe the same candidate state.\n\n---\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nSQLite (live store)                    issues.jsonl (git artifact)\n    │                                        │\n    │  all reads and writes                  │  exported by bf sync --flush\n    │                                        │  imported by bf sync --import\n    ▼                                        ▼\n  beads.db                            .beads/issues.jsonl\n  (gitignored)                        (committed to git)\n```\n\n**SQLite is the live database.** JSONL is a derived snapshot committed to git for backup, cross-machine sharing, and recovery. `beads.db` is rebuilt at any time with `bf doctor --repair`.\n\n**No daemon. No server. No flock.** Each `bf` invocation is self-contained: open DB, acquire write lock, execute, release, exit. SQLite WAL mode enables concurrent reads during writes.\n\n---\n\n## Key Features\n\n### Atomic Concurrent Claiming\n\n```\nWorker 1: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT + score → UPDATE winner → COMMIT\nWorker 2: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → (blocked until Worker 1 commits) → SELECT → ...\n...\nWorker 20: BEGIN IMMEDIATE → SELECT → NONE (queue empty) → COMMIT\n```\n\nThe entire claim pipeline — stale reclamation, candidate scoring, winner selection, status update, event recording — runs in a single SQLite write transaction. Guaranteed correct under any concurrency level.\n\n### Critical Path Scoring\n\n`bf` computes the **float** of each bead: how many hops it can slip before delaying its epic. Beads on the critical path (`float == 0`) receive a 1000-point bonus in the claim scorer, ensuring the fleet works on the most impactful beads automatically — without human priority curation.\n\n```\n$ bf critical-path bf-epic-123\n\n  float=0  [bf-a3f8] Implement auth token refresh        in_progress\n  float=0  [bf-b2c1] Fix concurrent session state bug    open\n  float=2  [bf-d5e3] Update API documentation            open\n```\n\n### Velocity-Aware Scoring\n\nWorkers declare their composition when claiming:\n\n```bash\nbf claim --assignee worker-7 \\\n         --model claude-sonnet-4-6 \\\n         --harness needle \\\n         --harness-version 0.5.2 \\\n         --json\n```\n\n`bf` tracks close times per `(model, harness, issue_type)` in `velocity_stats`. The claim scorer weights candidates by `impact / expected_duration` — the fleet maximizes throughput per unit time, not just raw priority. After 10+ completions per cohort, routing becomes measurably smarter.\n\n```\n$ bf velocity\n\nModel                  Harness   Type     Samples  p50    p90\nclaude-opus-4-7        needle    task     142      8m     22m\nclaude-sonnet-4-6      needle    task     87       18m    45m\nclaude-haiku-4-5       needle    task     23       35m    2h10m\n```\n\n### Atomic Batch Operations\n\nReplaces NEEDLE's crash-unsafe create+dep chains:\n\n```bash\n# NEEDLE mitosis: split 1 bead into N children atomically\n# Method 1: Dedicated mitosis command (recommended)\nbf mitosis bf-a3f8 \\\n  --children '[\n    {\"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2},\n    {\"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\", \"priority\": 2}\n  ]' \\\n  --reason \"Split into children\"\n\n# Method 2: Direct batch with placeholder references\n# Use @0, @1, ... to reference beads created earlier in the batch\nbf batch --json '[\n  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Implement login handler\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n  {\"op\": \"create\", \"title\": \"Add session tests\", \"type\": \"task\"},\n  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@0\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n  {\"op\": \"dep_add_blocker\", \"parent\": \"@1\", \"child\": \"bf-a3f8\"},\n  {\"op\": \"close\", \"id\": \"bf-a3f8\", \"reason\": \"Split into children\"}\n]'\n```\n\n**Placeholder references**: `@0`, `@1`, etc. resolve to the IDs of beads created earlier in the batch. You don't need to know the child IDs in advance — `bf` substitutes them automatically.\n\nAll operations execute in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. A crash mid-batch leaves zero partial state — SQLite rolls back automatically.\n\n### Extensible Annotations\n\nArbitrary key-value metadata on any bead, transparent to `br`:\n\n```bash\nbf annotate bf-a3f8 needle_attempt=3 needle_session=abc123 review_status=needs_review\nbf list --annotation needle_attempt=3\n```\n\n### Operation History\n\n```bash\nbf log bf-a3f8\n\n2026-04-29 10:00  CREATED     by: human                  \"Implement auth flow\"\n2026-04-29 14:30  CLAIMED     by: worker-3 (sonnet-4-6)  open → in_progress\n2026-04-29 15:45  COMMENT     by: worker-3               \"Found edge case\"\n2026-04-29 16:00  CLOSED      by: worker-3               \"Completed\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Secret Scanning (Pre-Commit Hook)\n\n`bf` scans for secrets before writing to the database (see `.beads/config.yaml` → `secret_protection`). The `bf commit-check` command extends this to git — it runs as a pre-commit hook to block commits that would add secrets to `.beads/` files.\n\n```bash\n# Manual scan\nbf commit-check\n# Exits 0 (clean) or 1 (secrets found) with detailed error output\n```\n\n**Installation as git pre-commit hook:**\n\n```bash\n# In your workspace repo\ncat > .git/hooks/pre-commit << 'EOF'\n#!/bin/sh\nbf commit-check\nEOF\n\nchmod +x .git/hooks/pre-commit\n```\n\n**What gets scanned:**\n- `.beads/config.yaml` — workspace configuration\n- `.beads/metadata.json` — database path settings\n- `.beads/issues.jsonl` — bead data (git artifact)\n\n**Patterns detected:**\n- AWS keys (`AKIA...`, secret access keys)\n- GitHub tokens (`ghp_`, `gho_`, `github_pat_`)\n- Private keys (`BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY`, `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY`)\n- JWT tokens\n- API keys in URLs\n- Database connection strings\n- And more (same scanner as write-time protection)\n\n**Allowlist exceptions:**\n\nIf a pattern is legitimately non-secret, add to `.beads/config.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\nsecret_protection:\n  allowlist:\n    - \"^AKIAEXAMPLE$\"  # exact string\n    - \"password: test\"  # substring\n```\n\n---\n\n## Commands\n\n```\nbf create        --title \"...\" --type <type> --priority <N> [--label ...]\nbf list          [--status <s>] [--type <t>] [--assignee <a>] [--format json|text|toon]\nbf show          <id>\nbf update        <id> [--status <s>] [--assignee <a>] ...]\nbf close         <id> [--reason \"...\"]\nbf claim         --assignee <id> [--model <m>] [--harness <h>] [--harness-version <v>]\n                 [--workspace <path>] [--any]\nbf critical-path <epic-id>\nbf batch         [--file ops.json] [--json '[...]']\nbf annotate      <id> key=value [--remove key]\nbf log           [<id>] [--since <date>] [--actor <a>] [--diff]\nbf rotate        [--age-days N]\nbf velocity      [--model <m>] [--harness <h>]\nbf dep           add-blocker <blocker> <blockee>\nbf dep           tree <id>\nbf label         <id> add <label> [<label> ...]\nbf sync          [--flush-only] [--import-only]\nbf doctor        [--repair] [--check] [--reclaim-stale [--ttl <duration>]]\nbf init          [--prefix <p>]\nbf stats\nbf search        <query>\nbf commit-check  # git pre-commit hook for secret scanning\n```\n\nAll `br` commands work identically. `bf` is a strict superset.\n\n---\n\n## NEEDLE Integration\n\nReplace the five non-atomic `br` chains in `bead_store/mod.rs`:\n\n| Old (racy) | New (atomic) |\n|-----------|--------------|\n| `br ready` → `br update` → `br show` (3 calls, 2 race windows) | `bf claim` (1 call, 1 transaction) |\n| `br create` + `br dep add` (orphan if crash between) | `bf batch` (crash-safe, all-or-nothing) |\n| `br show` just to get `.labels` field | `bf labels <id>` (direct `SELECT` on labels table) |\n| N × `br label add` loops (N processes) | `bf label <id> add l1 l2 l3` (one transaction) |\n\nBackward compatibility — install `br` as a symlink:\n\n```bash\nln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n```\n\n---\n\n## Compatibility\n\n`bf` reads and writes the same SQLite schema and JSONL format as `br`. A workspace used by `bf` can be read by `br` and vice versa. Verified by three test suites (see plan §Compatibility Verification):\n\n1. **JSONL round-trip**: `br list --format json` == `bf list --format json` on the same workspace\n2. **SQLite compat**: `bf` writes, `br` reads, `br doctor` passes\n3. **Claim race**: 20 concurrent `bf claim` processes, 10 beads — exactly 10 claims succeed, no bead claimed twice\n\n---\n\n## Reliability\n\n- **rusqlite** (standard SQLite C library) — not FrankenSQLite, which had known corruption under concurrent writes\n- **WAL mode** — concurrent reads never block writes; writers queue, not corrupt\n- **`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`** — acquires write lock before any reads; eliminates TOCTOU\n- **`SQLITE_BUSY` retry** — exponential backoff up to 5 retries; converts contention spikes to short waits\n- **JSONL always authoritative** — `bf doctor --repair` rebuilds `beads.db` from `issues.jsonl` at any time\n\n---\n\n## Implementation\n\nSee [`docs/plan/plan.md`](plan/plan.md) for the complete implementation plan including:\n\n- Exact SQLite schema (all 13 tables + indexes)\n- `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` retry wrapper implementation\n- Critical path CTE algorithm\n- Velocity stats schema and claim scoring formula\n- Compatibility verification test suites\n- NEEDLE integration specifics (file, line numbers, before/after)\n\n---\n\n## Migration from br to bf\n\n### Per-Machine Installation\n\n```bash\n# Install bf binary\ncurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n\n# Drop-in replace br (all existing scripts work unchanged)\nln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n\n# Verify installation\nbf --version\nbr --version  # should show same version\n```\n\n### Per-Workspace Migration\n\n**Standard migration** (Path B: explicit, with backup and verification):\n\n```bash\n# Migrate a single workspace\nbf migrate --workspace /path/to/workspace\n\n# Or migrate all workspaces in a loop\nfor workspace in \\\n  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n  bf migrate --workspace \"$workspace\"\ndone\n```\n\n**What `bf migrate` does:**\n1. Acquires migration lock (prevents concurrent claims during migration)\n2. Backs up `beads.db` → `beads.db.br-backup-<timestamp>`\n3. Applies schema migrations (creates bf-only tables via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`)\n4. Primes critical_path_cache for all epics\n5. Seeds `config.yaml` with bf-specific defaults if missing\n6. Verifies forward compatibility (br can still open the database)\n7. Verifies backward compatibility (`bf doctor --check` passes)\n8. Releases migration lock\n\n**Dry-run mode** (see what would happen without making changes):\n\n```bash\nbf migrate --workspace /path/to/workspace --dry-run\n```\n\n**Recovery mode** (for corrupted/missing databases):\n\nIf `beads.db` is corrupted or missing, `bf migrate --from-jsonl` rebuilds from `issues.jsonl` and reconstructs events from git history:\n\n```bash\nbf migrate --workspace /path/to/workspace --from-jsonl [--seed-velocity]\n```\n\n### Migration Checklist\n\n**Step 1: Per-machine installation**\n\n```bash\n# Install bf binary\ncurl -L https://github.com/jedarden/bead-forge/releases/latest/download/bf-linux-x86_64 \\\n  -o ~/.local/bin/bf && chmod +x ~/.local/bin/bf\n\n# Drop-in replace br (all existing scripts work unchanged)\nln -sf ~/.local/bin/bf ~/.local/bin/br\n\n# Verify installation\nbf --version\nbr --version  # should show same version\n```\n\n**Step 2: Per-workspace migration loop**\n\n```bash\n# Migrate all workspaces\nfor workspace in \\\n  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n  echo \"Migrating $workspace...\"\n  bf migrate --workspace \"$workspace\"\ndone\n```\n\n**Step 3: Verify each migration**\n\n```bash\n# Verify each workspace passes both doctor checks\nfor workspace in \\\n  /home/coding/FORGE \\\n  /home/coding/NEEDLE \\\n  /home/coding/AgentScribe \\\n  /home/coding/ARMOR \\\n  /home/coding/SIGIL \\\n  /home/coding/CLASP \\\n  /home/coding/bead-forge; do\n  echo \"Checking $workspace...\"\n  cd \"$workspace\" || continue\n  echo \"  bf doctor:\"\n  bf doctor || echo \"  ❌ bf doctor failed\"\n  echo \"  br doctor:\"\n  br doctor || echo \"  ❌ br doctor failed\"\ndone\n```\n\nBoth `bf doctor` and `br doctor` should exit 0 with no errors for each workspace.\n\n**Step 4: Update NEEDLE adapter configs**\n\nAfter migration, update NEEDLE adapter configs to pass worker metadata for velocity tracking:\n\n```yaml\n# In .config/needle/adapters/claude-sonnet.yaml, update invoke_template:\nbf claim --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --harness-version 0.5.2 ...\n```\n\nThis enables velocity-aware routing (see §Velocity-Aware Scoring above).\n\n### Verification After Migration\n\nFor each workspace, verify both tools can read the database:\n\n```bash\n# Verify bf doctor passes\ncd /path/to/workspace\nbf doctor --check\n\n# Verify br doctor passes (forward compatibility)\nbr doctor\n```\n\nBoth commands should exit 0 with no errors.\n\n**Known limitation:** The migration may show a forward compatibility warning: \"Forward compatibility check failed: issues table column count mismatch\". This is expected because bf adds a `content_hash` column to the issues table for sync optimization. The database remains fully functional — both `bf doctor` and `br doctor` will pass. The warning indicates that br sees an extra column, but all br operations continue to work correctly.\n\n### NEEDLE Integration Update\n\nAfter migration, update NEEDLE adapter configs to pass worker metadata for velocity tracking:\n\n```yaml\n# In .config/needle/adapters/claude-sonnet.yaml, update invoke_template:\nbf claim --model claude-sonnet-4-6 --harness needle --harness-version 0.5.2 ...\n```\n\nThis enables velocity-aware routing (see §Velocity-Aware Scoring above).\n\n---\n\n## Build & Deploy\n\nBuilt via Argo Workflows on `iad-ci`.
